# Implementation record: feature 29 `web_app` (phase 16, `sdd: false`, full group: security)

## Stage 1 of 4: the BFF, auth, the shell, generated types, and the error-text instrument

Implementer: Claude (Sonnet 5.5), 2026-10-15. Feature 29 stays `in_progress` (the leader owns `feature_list.json`; I did not edit it). Nothing was committed.

Specification of record: `feature_list.json` id 29's seven acceptance items (counted: 7) and `specs/shared/openapi.yaml`. This stage owns items 1 and 2 in full, item 4's instrument (built now, with the login page and the signed-in shell as its first population), and from item 6 the stack label `#9 · Python / Angular` and `WEB_PORT=3010`. Items 3, 5 (all pages but login) and 7 are stages 2 to 4.

There is no `R<n>` for this feature (`sdd: false`; `specs/shared/test-matrix.md` has no web rows and `specs/**` is outside my bounds, so I did not touch it). Tests carry local claim tags instead, used in their names: `R-session`, `R-relay`, `R-local`, `R-csrf`, `R-stream`; section 7 maps each acceptance item to its tests.

### 1. What was built

**BFF** (`apps/web/src/server/**`, Nitro with h3 1.15.11, framework imported explicitly, never through Nitro auto-imports):

- `lib/config.ts` (the only module that reads `process.env`), `lib/session.ts` (h3 `useSession`), `lib/gateway.ts` (the only module that calls the Gateway: `callGateway`, `relay`, `proxyToGateway`, BFF-local `notSignedIn` / `gatewayFailed`), `lib/stream-proxy.ts`, `lib/csrf.ts`, `lib/problem.ts`.
- 15 route files under `routes/api/**` plus a catch-all `routes/api/[...path].ts` (a 404 problem in the BFF's own words, because Nitro's client renderer would otherwise answer an unknown `/api` path with `index.html` and status 200). `middleware/csrf.ts`, `plugins/validate-config.ts` (fails the boot on a short `WEB_SESSION_PASSWORD`).
- SSR is OFF (`analog({ ssr: false, prerender: { routes: [] } })`): every page sits behind a cookie session whose data comes from the BFF, so nothing is server-rendered and there is no Angular `TransferState` to carry state into a page. The Nitro server still runs, as the BFF and as the static host. (`prerender.routes: []` stops Analog's default prerender of `/`, which needs the SSR entry.) `main.server.ts`, `app.config.server.ts`, the welcome page and the hello route of the scaffold were removed.

**Client shell** (`apps/web/src/app/**`): zoneless Angular 22 with signals, Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/vite` 4.3.3), spartan/ui 1.7.0 generated into `src/app/ui` (button, input, label, card, alert, badge, table + utils; `components.json` present, artifacts checked), TanStack Query for Angular 5.104.2 (exact). `api/api-client.ts` is the single seam (the `API_FETCH` token + a typed method per BFF route); `session/session.ts` (query options + route guard), `session/signed-out.ts` (QueryClient whose caches send any 401 to `/login`), `lib/problem.ts`, `lib/stack-label.ts` (the one definition), `lib/title-strategy.ts`, `pages/login.page.ts`, `pages/(app).page.ts` (signed-in shell, guard), `pages/(app)/orders.page.ts` (a placeholder so the shell has a signed-in page; stage 2 replaces it), `pages/index.page.ts` (redirect).

**Generated types**: `scripts/openapi-types.mjs` (ported from #8, `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0 exact) -> `src/generated/openapi.ts` (checked in, 1696 lines); `src/shared/api-types.ts` is its only importer; `types:generate` / `types:check` scripts; `types:check` added to `quality.sh` section 7 (one line plus its comment, between `lint` and `test:ci`).

**Port**: `scripts/serve.mjs` (`dev` and `preview` read `WEB_PORT`, default 3010, after the repository `.env` has been loaded by `node --env-file-if-exists`).

**The instrument** (item 4): `src/app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` + `src/app/testing/{browser-harness,page-sweep}.ts` for the browser pages; `src/server/routes.sweep.spec.ts` for the BFF routes.

**Tests** (Vitest 5, two projects in `vite.config.ts`: `server` in Node, `app` in jsdom with the Angular test bed):

| file | project | what it proves |
|---|---|---|
| `src/server/bff.spec.ts` (+ `testing/bff-contract.ts`) | server | the BFF contract in-process over h3 on a real socket, plus env and session variants |
| `src/build/production-bff.spec.ts` | server | the same contract against `node dist/analog/server/index.mjs` after a fresh `vite build`, plus boot failure, route population, SPA shell, bundle and page scan |
| `src/server/routes.sweep.spec.ts` | server | observe-then-fail-each upstream call of every route |
| `src/server/boundaries.spec.ts`, `src/server/config.spec.ts` | server | where secrets live; configuration functions |
| `scripts/openapi-types.spec.ts`, `scripts/serve.spec.ts`, `scripts/typecheck.spec.ts` | server | type drift check (armed in a copy), WEB_PORT, `tsc` over everything |
| `src/shared/generated-types.spec.ts` | server | one importer of the generated types |
| `src/app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` | app | the behavioural sweep of the browser pages |
| `src/app/login.spec.ts`, `session.spec.ts`, `api/api-client.spec.ts`, `lib/problem.spec.ts`, `seam.spec.ts`, `stack-label.spec.ts`, `app-config.spec.ts`, `ui-components.spec.ts` | app | page behaviour, guard and 401, API seam, problem text, label, providers, spartan and TanStack artifacts |

### 2. Route set decision

The BFF exposes 15 routes: `POST /api/auth/login`, `POST /api/auth/logout`, `GET /api/auth/session`, `GET /api/catalog/{products,retailers,companies}`, `GET /api/credits`, `GET /api/invoices`, `POST /api/invoices/{id}/payments`, `GET /api/orders`, `POST /api/orders`, `GET /api/orders/{id}`, `GET /api/orders/stream`, `GET /api/stock`, `POST /api/stock/replenish`. This is the set of both predecessors: #7 has 15 files under `server/api/**` (`find` output: auth x3, catalog x3, credits, invoices x2, orders x4, stock x2) and #8 has the same set with the three catalog routes folded into `app/api/catalog/[kind]/route.ts` (13 `route.ts` files). **`/orders/{id}/cancel` (openapi.yaml line 342) is exposed by neither** (`ls` of `../order-to-cash-nestjs/apps/web/server/api/orders` and `../order-to-cash-dotnet/apps/web/src/app/api/orders`: no cancel route), and no acceptance item of feature 29 asks the UI for a cancel control, so #9 does not expose it either. If a later stage adds a cancel button it adds a route file, and `routes.sweep.spec.ts` then fails by name ("route files with no INVOCATIONS entry") until the route has a canonical request and its reviewed upstream calls. The catalogue is three literal files (not a `[kind]` parameter), so no other path under `/catalog` can be proxied. Population: `ROUTE_FILES` (glob) and `routeFilesOnDisk()` (directory walk) are compared in the sweep.

### 3. Settings read (for the leader's `.env.example`)

| name | default | read where | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GATEWAY_BASE_URL` | unset | `server/lib/config.ts` `gatewayBaseUrl()` | wins when set (trailing slashes dropped); a blank value is unset |
| `GATEWAY_PORT` | `3001` | same | used only to build `http://localhost:${GATEWAY_PORT}` when `GATEWAY_BASE_URL` is unset; already in `.env.example` (line 243) |
| `WEB_SESSION_PASSWORD` | unset | `server/lib/config.ts` `sessionPassword()` | at least 32 characters; set but shorter: the server refuses to boot (`plugins/validate-config.ts`) and every login would fail closed; unset: a random per-process key with one warning (sessions end when the server restarts) |
| `WEB_COOKIE_SECURE` | on | `server/lib/config.ts` `cookieSecure()` | only the exact value `false` turns `Secure` off (browsers treat `http://localhost` as a secure context, so the default works locally) |
| `WEB_GATEWAY_TIMEOUT_MS` | `15000` | `server/lib/config.ts` `gatewayTimeoutMs()` | deadline for a proxied Gateway call (not for the SSE stream); a timeout is the BFF's own 504 problem |
| `WEB_PORT` | `3010` | `scripts/serve.mjs` | `dev` and `preview`/`start`; a variable exported in the shell wins over the `.env` file; `.env` wins over the default. Nothing in `apps/web` names port 3000 (`scripts/serve.spec.ts` walks the tree) |

The leader should NOT write `WEB_PORT=3000` into `.env.example` (#8 L1). Probed with node 24.19.0 and the real `scripts/serve.mjs`: `.env` alone with `WEB_PORT=4555` gives port 4555; the same `.env` with `WEB_PORT=4777` exported in the shell gives 4777; neither gives 3010. All server settings are read at request time from the environment; none is a public/runtime value and none reaches the client bundle (`boundaries.spec.ts`; production bundle scan in `production-bff.spec.ts`).

### 5. The ported-idiom ledger

Format: "#7 relied on X (file:line); #8 supplied it with Y (file:line); in #9 it is Z, guarded by <test>, armed <arm>".

| idiom | #7 | #8 | #9 |
|---|---|---|---|
| sealed session cookie | h3 `useSession` in `server/utils/session.ts:29-40`, `secure: true` hard-coded | `iron-session` `sealData`, attributes set on every response by `session.ts:36-59`, `secure: cookieSecure()` | h3 `useSession` with attributes written in `server/lib/session.ts` `cookieAttributes()` (not library defaults) plus an explicit `maxAge`; contract "login answers identity only and seals the JWT into an httpOnly SameSite=Lax Secure cookie", in-process and production; B01, B02 |
| h3 reads the session from a request header | not noticed | n/a (iron-session) | **new hazard**: `useSession` also unseals `x-<name>-session` unless `sessionHeader: false` (`node_modules/h3/dist/index.mjs:1370`); set to `false`; contract "default session header is not an authentication path" sends a garbage cookie so the request reaches h3; B05 |
| anonymous requests | h3 mints and `Set-Cookie`s an empty session for every cookie-less request | n/a | `readSession` returns `undefined` before touching h3 when there is no cookie; contract "anonymous request must not mint a cookie" |
| clearing the cookie | `session.clear()` (h3 `clearSession`: empty value, no `Max-Age`, `index.mjs:1469`) | `maxAge: 0` (`session.ts:58`) | `deleteCookie` with the same attributes: `Max-Age=0`; contract logout and 401 cases; B07 |
| session password unset | hard-coded dev password (`nuxt.config.ts:34`) | random per-process key on `globalThis`, throws under 32 chars (`config.ts:25-43`) | #8's rule, plus a boot-time check (`plugins/validate-config.ts`); on `globalThis` for the same reason as #8 (one key across module instances); `config.spec.ts`; production "refuses to boot"; B18 (the in-process variant is backed by h3's own 32-character check, so B18 arms the production boot test) |
| Gateway problem relay | `createError({ data })` -> Nitro wraps it one level deeper (`server/utils/gateway.ts:12-22`) | `NextResponse` bytes verbatim (`gateway.ts:62-72`) | `relay` writes the upstream `Uint8Array`, status and four named headers through h3 (no `createError` anywhere); `routes.sweep.spec.ts` + captured real Gateway bodies byte for byte; B08, B20 |
| 2xx statuses that matter (202 + `Retry-After`, 200 + `Idempotent-Replay`) | `gatewayFetchWithStatus` (`server/utils/gateway.ts:65-85`) | `relay` forwards status and headers | `relay`; contract "status, body bytes and headers are relayed"; E04 (Idempotency-Key) |
| browser-supplied headers | n/a | explicit allowlist `forwardRequestHeaders` | same allowlist; `Authorization` is set last by the BFF; contract "a browser-supplied Authorization header is never forwarded"; B16, B17 |
| SSE: open upstream | `fetch` + `sendWebResponse` (`stream.get.ts:31-43`) | `fetch` + a `ReadableStream` wrapper (`stream-proxy.ts:43-65`) | `fetch` + a hand-built pump to `event.node.res` (`pumpStream`) |
| SSE: client closes -> upstream aborted | `event.node.req.on('close')` (`stream.get.ts:29`) | request `signal` abort + body `cancel` (`stream/route.ts:25`, `stream-proxy.ts:61-63`) | **engine-supplied vs hand-built cleanup**: h3's `sendStream` on a web stream is `pipeTo` with no close handling (`index.mjs:866-876`), so the abort is hand-built on the RESPONSE's `close`. Probed on Node 24.19.0 (scratch script): for a body-less GET the request's `close` and the response's `close` both fire at the client's disconnect (305 ms for a destroy at 300 ms), so #7's choice was sound for this route; response `close` was chosen as the lifecycle of the thing streamed. Contract "closing the client connection aborts the upstream within a bounded time" (3 s bound), in-process and production; B09 |
| SSE: nothing held back | n/a (Nuxt) | a `: connected` preamble because Next writes status and headers with the first chunk (`stream-proxy.ts:29-35`) | `writeHead` + `flushHeaders` before the first read, so no preamble; no compression in Nitro's node-server for dynamic responses (contract sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br` and requires no `content-encoding`); lockstep test: each of 5 frames is awaited at the client before the next is written upstream; B11 |
| SSE: reconnect | n/a | real `EventSource` through the production server (`tests-integration/stream-proxy.test.ts:245`) | the `eventsource` package (5.1.1, as #8) in the contract: the Gateway drops the first connection, the second request carries `Last-Event-ID: c1` |
| port 0 | n/a | n/a | **new finding**: Nitro treats `NITRO_PORT=0` as unset and binds 3000 (taken on this machine). Every earlier production run of my own suite had silently used 3000. `startServer` now asks the OS for a port, passes it, and rejects when the "Listening on" line names another port |
| CSRF | `SameSite=Lax` only | `SameSite=Lax` only (no origin check, `grep -ri csrf src` empty) | `SameSite=Lax` plus `middleware/csrf.ts`: every non-read method under `/api` is refused when `Sec-Fetch-Site` is not `same-origin`/`none` or `Origin` is not this host; requests carrying neither header (curl, servers) pass because a browser always sends `Origin` on a cross-origin POST; the BFF reads JSON, so a cross-site form cannot carry credentials either. Contract "R-csrf" (refused both ways, reads never refused, login CSRF); B12, B13, B14 |
| typed wire shapes | `packages/contracts` generate/check `.mts` | `scripts/openapi-types.mjs` with `openapi-typescript` 7.13.0 | the same script, same tool and pin, ported; one importer; `types:check` armed three ways (D03 hand edit; the spec's own one-line mutation in a COPY; missing artifact) |
| query/state library | `@tanstack/vue-query` | `@tanstack/react-query` 5.103.0 | `@tanstack/angular-query-experimental` 5.104.2, exact pin (checked against the lockfile in `ui-components.spec.ts`) |
| 401 -> sign in | middleware `auth.global.ts` + no 401 handling in queries | `QueryCache`/`MutationCache` `onError` (`providers.tsx:19-28`) | `SignedOutNavigator` on both caches; C14-C18 |
| SSR / transfer state | Nuxt payload | Next server components | **new in #9**, and absent by design: SSR off. Guard: production test requests `/`, `/orders`, `/login` with a session cookie and searches for the JWT and for `ng-state`/`TransferState`/JSON scripts; scans every file of `dist/client` for the JWT, the session password and server setting names; E01, E02 |
| Nitro auto-imports | relied on (`defineEventHandler`, `useRuntimeConfig`, `createError` with no import) | n/a | explicit `h3` imports; `boundaries.spec.ts` fails a use without an import (the type checker and the tests cannot see auto-imports) |
| TS integer/JSON/loop questions | n/a | n/a | none apply to this stage: no money arithmetic (stage 2), `JSON.parse` only on problem bodies, no timestamp formatting, no engine/pool affinity |

### 8. The arming table

Protocol (CLAUDE.md): every arm backs the touched file up into `.arm/web29s1/backup/<id>/` with its `sha256`, applies ONE mutation (or creates one new file), runs the ONE named test under `setsid --wait timeout -k 10 420 npx vitest run ...`, reads the verdict from the JSON reporter's artifact (`out_<id>.json`), restores with `cp`, confirms with `cmp`, clears `node_modules/.vite` and `.angular`, and logs to `.arm/web29s1/results.jsonl`. The driver is `.arm/web29s1/arm.py`, the arm definitions are `make_bff_arms.py`, `make_app_arms.py` (git-ignored). Arming never deleted or moved a tracked path: arms that create a file create a new one and remove it. A production-build arm rebuilds `dist/`, so `dist/` was rebuilt from the restored source before the live check and the final gate.

Result: **62 arms (counted: 25 BFF `B01-B25`, 24 client `C01-C24`, 4 port/types `D01-D04`, 9 build/bundle/sentinel `E01-E09`), 62 failed at the intended test, 62 restored byte-identical (`cmp`)** (arms that only create a file have nothing to restore; the file is removed). The verbatim-message column is the first 260 characters of the failure message as the JSON reporter records it (vitest prints a long actual value with an ellipsis there; the sweeps raise `expect.fail` with the full list of failures, which the console shows in full). `sha256` is the first 12 hex digits of the backup (full digests are in `results.jsonl`).

What arming changed in the tests (each is a guard that would otherwise have passed on a wrong reason):

- B05: the first header test sent no cookie, so my own "no cookie, no session" shortcut answered before h3's header lookup ran; the test now sends a garbage cookie with a valid sealed header, and fails without `sessionHeader: false`.
- B18: in-process the mutant is equivalent (h3 enforces its own 32-character minimum), so the arm targets the production boot test, which only my check can fail.
- C18: the 401-on-`/login` test could not tell navigation to `/login` from `/login` apart; it now asserts the cache is not cleared.
- B03/B17/B19/B07/B23-B25 and the sweeps: assertions gained messages that name the claim (vitest printed a bare "expected X to deeply equal Y" or a TypeError on `undefined`).
- B21: a route file with no sweep entry crashed the suite at collection time with "Cannot read properties of undefined"; it now yields a named failing test.
- B12/D02: two of my own first mutants did not compile (unreachable code; TypeScript syntax in `.mjs`) and "failed" for the wrong reason; replaced.
- C06/C07/C12/E07: the first `-t` patterns matched no test (vitest joins the describe path with ` > `, so `a b` never matched `a > b`): the artifact showed `passed 0, failed 0`, which is how this was noticed. An exit code, or a "no failure" in a log, would not have shown it.

### 9. Defeat-list rows (CLAUDE.md), by guard

| row | applies | where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 delete the behaviour | yes | B07, B09, B16, C14, C16, C20, C21, C22, E04, E06 |
| 2 corrupt a supplied field | yes | B03 (token field in the body), B10, B20 (body rewritten), C19 |
| 3 substitute a valid sibling identifier | yes | C19 (`#8 · .NET / Next.js` for `#9 · Python / Angular`), D01 (3000 for 3010), B02 (`none` for `lax`), E03 (decoded id for encoded id), `config.spec.ts` (`GATEWAY_PORT` vs sibling `*_PORT`) |
| 4 shadow the pattern in a comment or string | yes | `seam.spec.ts` and `boundaries.spec.ts` strip comments with the TypeScript printer, so prose naming `fetch` or `accessToken` is not a use of it (these files' own comments name them and pass); strings stay |
| 5 hide it in a dead region | partly | the scans report presence anywhere, so a use inside `if (false)` is flagged (fails closed); B14 and B12 use `false as boolean` / `true as boolean` to keep a branch dead and the behavioural test still notices |
| 6 hide it in a raw or triple-quoted string | partly | template literals survive the printer and are scanned, so a primitive in a template is flagged (fails closed) |
| 7 drop an optional element | yes | B05 (`sessionHeader`), E04 (`forwardRequestHeaders`), C03 (title branch), the `title`-only variant of every sweep |
| 8 compare a literal to a literal | guarded | every `EXPECTED_*` literal is compared with a population derived from the disk, the router or the generated types, never with another literal; `failureStatuses` is a literal list compared with statuses derived from `openapi.yaml` (C08) |
| 9 satisfy the closer half and leave the premise stale | yes | E08 (a stale/wrong `dist/`), D03 (generated file stale against the spec), the production suite always runs `vite build` first |
| 10 let build output or caches join the population | yes | walks exclude `node_modules`, `dist`, `.angular`, `generated`, `app/ui`, `app/testing` BY PATH at the walk; the bundle scan reads `dist/client` after a fresh build on purpose |
| 11 write it in a form the instrument does not recognise (test the behaviour) | yes | C07 (early-return shape), C12 (direct `globalThis.fetch`, found by the stubbed global at run time, not by the scan), B04 (a second cookie), B11 (buffering) |
| 12 serve the failure through a path the population never drives | yes | login's second upstream call (B08), BFF-local 502/504 (C08), the SSE reconnect, `default` responses drawn as 500, interaction-gated reads (`REQUEST_UNITS`, C10); the no-JavaScript form path does not exist in a client-rendered app |

Instrument change: the sweep's interaction helpers were swapped for Testing Library's queries (`getByLabelText`, `getByRole`, `fireEvent.input`). New premises: a field must have an associated `<label>` and a button an accessible name (a page that loses either now fails the script by name instead of silently typing nowhere); both C-group run after the swap.

### 10. Live record (2026-10-15)

Before: `docker ps` showed only `otcpy-n8n`. Started `docker compose -p otcpy -f docker-compose.infra.yml start postgres mongodb kafka nats`, waited for `otcpy-kafka` healthy, started the Gateway with `uv run python -m otc_gateway` (`/health/ready` 200, port 3001), built the web app, and started it exactly as the scripts do: `node --env-file-if-exists=../../.env scripts/serve.mjs preview` (no `WEB_PORT` anywhere, so `Listening on http://[::]:3010`; `WEB_SESSION_PASSWORD` unset, so the random-key warning printed). Real Chromium (playwright-core 1.63.0 from #8's checkout, cached browser) drove it; the script and its outputs were scratch files, not added to the repository.

| check | result |
|---|---|
| `/` in a browser | redirected to `/login`; title `Sign in · Order-To-Cash · #9 · Python / Angular`; the login page shows `#9 · Python / Angular` |
| a wrong password | the alert says `Bad credentials.`: the real Gateway's `detail` (its `title` is `Bad credentials`, without the period, so this is the detail, not the title) |
| sign in | lands on `/orders`; title `Orders · Order-To-Cash · #9 · Python / Angular`; header text `Order-To-Cash #9 · Python / Angular Orders Order-To-Cash Operator Log out` |
| the cookie as Chromium holds it | one cookie, `otc_session`: `httpOnly: true`, `secure: true`, `sameSite: Lax`, `path: /`, expires in 43200 s, 774 characters, does not contain the token; `document.cookie` is the empty string |
| the JWT | the Gateway's token was obtained with `curl` (208 characters). Searched in 27 places (rendered HTML, `localStorage` + `sessionStorage`, the 8 JS/CSS assets the page loaded, the 15 response bodies the page received, the cookie value, `document.cookie`): 0 hits for the token, 0 hits for the JWT header segment `eyJ...` that every Gateway token shares, 0 hits for a JWT-shaped string. The session's own token is sealed, and each login issues a new one, so the header-segment and shape searches are what stands in for it; the shape regex was confirmed to match the curl token. No `ng-state` / `TransferState` in the page. `GET /api/auth/session` with the cookie answers `{"authenticated":true,"username":"operator","displayName":"Order-To-Cash Operator","roles":["operator"]}` |
| one SSE frame | a real `EventSource('/api/orders/stream')` in the page received `stream.ready` with `{"cursor":"1792040129108-4","resumed":false,"orderId":null}`; `curl -N` with the session cookie received the same frame |
| a closed tab aborts the upstream | established connections from the web process to `:3001` (`ss -tnp`): fresh server 0; after login 2 (pooled keep-alive sockets that expire on their own after about 4 to 6 s); with the stream open 1 at 1.5 s and still 1 at 7.5 s (so the 1 is the stream); 1.5 s after the client went away 0. In the browser run: before 0, during 1, 1.5 s after `page.close()` 0 |
| development server | `WEB_PORT=3011 node --env-file-if-exists=../../.env scripts/serve.mjs dev` answered `/api/auth/session`, `/login` 200, an unknown `/api` path as a 404 problem, and relayed the real Gateway's 401 for a wrong password |

The first live attempt FAILED and found a defect no earlier test could: the `dist/` that the production spec had just built (inside Vitest) was broken, because Analog's Nitro plugin takes its test-mode branch when `VITEST` / `NODE_ENV=test` are in the environment, publishes `src/server` as the static directory, and the server answered `/` with the SOURCE `index.html` (`/src/main.ts`): the app never started in a browser, while every API assertion of the production suite passed. Fixed in `buildProduction()` (explicit environment), guarded by a new production test that follows the served page's own `<script src>` and requires the built `index.html` with a JavaScript asset (`production-bff.spec.ts`, "the page the server answers is the BUILT one"), armed by E08. Two stray production servers from my own earlier test runs were also found holding ports (one on 3000); see the surprises.

After: Gateway and web stopped, `docker compose ... stop postgres mongodb kafka nats`; `docker ps` shows only `otcpy-n8n`; ports 3000, 3001, 3010 and 3011 are free; no `vite`, `otc_gateway` or `dist/analog` process left.

### 13. Surprises, open points for the reviewer, and what I did not do

**Surprises**

1. **Nitro treats `NITRO_PORT=0` as unset and binds 3000.** Every production run my own suite made used port 3000 until something else took it; `ss` then showed a stray production server of mine on 3000. Fixed: the port is asked of the OS and the "Listening on" line must name it; a registry of started servers (`liveServers()`) is asserted empty after the suite (armed by E09), and a server that fails to come up is killed. Two strays from earlier runs were killed by hand.
2. **The production `dist/` built under Vitest was broken** (see section 10). The API assertions passed against it; only a real browser noticed.
3. **h3's `useSession` reads the session from a request header by default and mints a cookie for every anonymous request.** Both neutralised (section 5).
4. **The Angular plugin type-checks test files during the transform**, so a type error in a spec shows as a failed suite, not a failed test; `scripts/typecheck.spec.ts` runs `tsc` over everything because the BFF is not compiled by the Angular build.
5. **Analog specifics**: a redirect page exports `routeMeta` and no component; `@analogjs/router` must be inlined in Vitest (`server.deps.inline`) or `provideFileRouter()` sees no pages; the pages must be in `tsconfig.spec.json`'s program or they are transformed without the Angular compiler; `ssr: false` still prerenders `/` unless `prerender.routes: []`.
6. **Vitest's `-t` joins with ` > `**; and its JSON reporter truncates long actual values (see section 8).
7. **Probe of #7's assumption**: Node 24.19's request `close` does fire at disconnect for a GET, so #7's `req.on('close')` was sound; I did not repeat the claim I first wrote in a comment (that it fires early), which the probe disproved.

**Open points (for the Opus reviewer or a gate)**

- **CSRF stance** goes beyond both predecessors (an `Origin` / `Sec-Fetch-Site` check on every non-read `/api` request). Requests with neither header pass by design. If the maintainer prefers #7/#8's cookie-only stance, delete `middleware/csrf.ts` and the `R-csrf` case.
- **SSR is off**, which removes the `TransferState` path rather than guarding it. The guard that would catch someone re-enabling it is the production page/bundle scan (E01).
- **Nitro binds all interfaces** (`Listening on http://[::]:3010`). The Gateway does too (`GATEWAY_HOST=0.0.0.0` in `.env.example`), so I left it; `NITRO_HOST` would narrow it.
- **The sweep is jsdom, not a browser.** The live check covered the login page and the shell in Chromium; the full-page sweep for stages 2 and 3 stays in jsdom unless a stage-4 Playwright pass is added. Playwright is not a dependency of `apps/web` yet (the live check borrowed #8's cached `playwright-core`).
- **`LABELS` and `NOT_SHOWN` are empty**, so the "every reviewed label is produced" check is vacuous today; it is armed only on the "extra text" side (C04). Stage 2 fills both and should arm the stale-label direction.
- **`/orders` is a placeholder page** (the shell needs one signed-in page to be tested); stage 2 replaces it and the `EXPECTED_*` tables.
- The `quality.sh` run of `types:check` happens before `test:ci`; the production suite rebuilds `dist/` inside `test:ci` and `build` rebuilds it again (about 7 s each).

**Not done (and why)**

- `specs/shared/test-matrix.md` has no web rows and `specs/**` is read-only for this stage; no matrix edit.
- `.env.example` untouched (the leader owns it; settings are in section 3).
- Stages 2 to 4 (money, orders pages, stock, billing, README, the inherited-id close-out) were not built.
- No `git` command was run; nothing is committed. `feature_list.json` was not edited.



## Stage 1 — fix round 1

Implementer: Claude (Sonnet 5.5), 2026-10-15. Inputs: `progress/brief_impl_web_app_stage1_round2.md`, `progress/review_web_app.md` "Stage 1 review". Feature 29 stays `in_progress`; I did not edit `feature_list.json`, `review_web_app.md`, `specs/**`, `services/**`, `packages/**`, `tests/**`, `.env.example`, `quality.sh`. Nothing is committed. `ss -ltnp` before and after: no Node listener of mine (the `nuxt dev` on 127.0.0.1:3000 and port 4000 were there before and are not mine).

### Changes, one row per item

| item | change | files | tests (new unless noted) |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 CSRF bypass | the middleware no longer tests the raw path: `csrfViolation` runs on every request (non-safe methods), and `unsupportedMediaType` refuses any mutation whose body is not `application/json` (a bodiless, header-less mutation such as logout passes) with the BFF's own 415 problem (`UNSUPPORTED_MEDIA_TYPE`); the false `readBody` comment is corrected | `src/server/middleware/csrf.ts`, `src/server/lib/csrf.ts` | contract `R-csrf: the check runs on every path the router can reach...` (`/%61pi/`, `/ap%69/`, `/%61%70%69/`; three header sets; login CSRF; same-site `text/plain` order), `R-csrf: a mutation that does not declare application/json is the BFF's own 415...` (urlencoded login, text/plain, same via encoded path, multipart, body with no Content-Type; bodiless logout and `Application/JSON; charset=utf-8` pass); each runs in-process (`bff.spec.ts`) AND on the production build (`production-bff.spec.ts`) |
| B2 SSE pre-header leak | the route creates the `AbortController` and attaches `res.on('close')` BEFORE `openUpstreamOrderStream`; after the await it checks `res.destroyed` / `signal.aborted`, cancels the body and returns; an aborted fetch error is not answered; `pumpStream` no longer owns the listener | `src/server/routes/api/orders/stream.get.ts`, `src/server/lib/stream-proxy.ts` | contract `R-stream: a client that leaves before the Gateway answers the stream's headers still has the upstream aborted within a bounded time` (in-process and production; the fake Gateway accepts and never answers headers; client destroys; `open.size` must reach 0 within 3000 ms) |
| B3 log guard blind to objects | new `testing/log-capture.ts`: every captured console call is rendered with `util.format` plus `util.inspect(arg, { depth: Infinity, maxArrayLength: null, maxStringLength: null })` per argument; both captures in `bff.spec.ts` use it; the production server's own output (`RunningServer.output()`) is asserted too | `src/server/testing/log-capture.ts`, `src/server/bff.spec.ts`, `src/build/production-bff.spec.ts` | `R-session: the JWT is in no log line...` (changed), `R-session: the JWT, its signature and the sealed cookie are in no line the production server writes...` (new) |
| B4 inline generic text | `labelled()` no longer exempts a node that contains the sentinel: the sentinel is removed, the remainder is trimmed of whitespace and punctuation and must be empty or a reviewed label (a node without the sentinel must itself be a label) | `src/app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` | the existing sweep tests (now catch the inline shape) + a unit premise `text sharing the problem's own text node is not exempt...` |
| B5 `..` ids | new `lib/path-id.ts` `pathId()` answers the BFF's own 400 (`VALIDATION_FAILED`) for an id that is empty, `.`, `..`, contains `/` or `?` once decoded (checked on the router param and on its second decode, because h3 leaves `%2F` encoded), or whose raw path holds `%3F`; both id routes use it | `src/server/lib/path-id.ts`, `routes/api/orders/[id].get.ts`, `routes/api/invoices/[id]/payments.post.ts` | contract `R-relay: an id that is empty, a dot segment, or holds a path or query separator once decoded is the BFF's own 400...` (12 refused targets sent through a raw socket because `fetch`/`URL` normalise `..` and `%2E%2E` away: `..`, `%2E%2E`, `%2e%2e`, `.`, `%2E`, `x%3Fy=1`, `..%2Fstock`, and the POST forms; a plain id `a%20b.c` reaches the Gateway as one encoded segment) |
| A1 tampered cookie | `readSession` unseals the cookie itself (`unsealSession`); an unreadable or past-lifetime cookie is cleared (`Max-Age=0`), never replaced by a freshly minted session | `src/server/lib/session.ts` | contract `R-session: a tampered cookie is no session` extended (tampered and `garbage`; no non-expiring `otc_session` is set; exactly one expiring line) |
| A2 Gateway URL in 502/504 | the `detail` no longer names the Gateway; `console.error` writes the URL and cause | `src/server/lib/gateway.ts` | contract 502 test extended; `bff.spec.ts` `R-local: an unreachable Gateway is answered without its address...` and the 504 test; production `R-local: an unreachable Gateway and a Gateway that does not answer...` (log checked on the output slice written by each request) |
| A5 h3 pin | `h3` is `1.15.11` in `package.json` (the version the lockfile resolved, confirmed in `pnpm-lock.yaml`), lockfile specifier edited to match; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` accepts it | `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` | `boundaries.spec.ts` `R-pin: h3 is an exact version ... and the lockfile resolves that same version` |
| A6 Origin | the check compares `new URL(origin).origin` with `${getRequestProtocol}://${getRequestHost}`; behind a TLS-terminating proxy the scheme is `https` only when it sends `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` (noted in the comment) | `src/server/lib/csrf.ts` | contract `R-csrf: the Origin check compares the whole origin...` (other scheme, other port, port-less; the identical origin passes) |

Also added: `rawRequest()` in `testing/bff-contract.ts` (a request whose target is sent exactly as given), `Bff.output` was not needed (production tests read `bff.server.output()`).

### B5 class enumeration

Command (tests and `testing/` excluded at the source by `grep -v`, which here only removes spec/helper paths):
`grep -rn "getRequestURL\|event\.path\|getRouterParam\|\.pathname\|originalUrl\|node\.req\.url" src/server scripts --include=*.ts --include=*.mjs | grep -v "\.spec\.ts\|/testing/"`

```
src/server/routes/api/[...path].ts:1:import { defineEventHandler, getRequestURL } from 'h3';
src/server/routes/api/[...path].ts:9:... ${getRequestURL(event).pathname} is not a route of this site.
src/server/lib/path-id.ts:1:import { getRequestURL, getRouterParam, type H3Event } from 'h3';
src/server/lib/path-id.ts:14:  const id = getRouterParam(event, name) ?? '';
src/server/lib/path-id.ts:16:  const rawPath = getRequestURL(event).pathname;
src/server/lib/gateway.ts:1:import { getHeader, getRequestURL, readRawBody, ... } from 'h3';
src/server/lib/gateway.ts:114:      search: method === 'GET' ? getRequestURL(event).search : '',
```

(`scripts/` has none.) Classification, one per hit: `[...path].ts:1,9` message text only (JSON-escaped into a problem detail), not a site; `path-id.ts:1,14,16` the fix itself (the only reader of a router param now; the two old reader sites, `orders/[id].get.ts:8` and `payments.post.ts:8`, no longer call `getRouterParam`); `gateway.ts:1` import; `gateway.ts:114` the raw GET `search`: not a site of the class, because it is appended after a path already built from a validated id, and a literal `?` is what starts it (a decoded `%3F` inside an id is refused by `pathId` and does not reach it); it is also the line the review named as the source of the `%3F` truncation: the truncation was h3's router cutting the decoded path at `?`, which `pathId` now refuses on the raw path. The CSRF site `middleware/csrf.ts:7` (the raw `pathname.startsWith('/api/')` test) is gone from the list: it was B1.

### Defeat-list rows, per new guard

- Contract `R-csrf` path forms / 415: row 1 (B1a/B1b delete the gate or the refusal), row 2 (corrupt: B1c refuses only `text/plain`), row 3 (sibling identifier: `/%61pi`, `/ap%69`, `/%61%70%69` against `/api`; scheme/port siblings in A6), row 7 (B1d drops the "body with no Content-Type" element), row 11 (the form the instrument did not recognise was the encoded path itself: the test sends raw targets).
- Stream pre-header: row 1 (B2c deletes the listener), row 12 (serves the failure through a path the population never drove: the pre-header window; B2a restores the original defect), a redundancy probe B2b (below).
- Log guard: row 11 (object arguments are the form `String()` did not see; B3a, B3b deeper than console prints, B3c in the production output), row 12 (the production server's own stdout/stderr is a path the console spies never drive).
- Sweep remainder: row 8 (the unit premise compares a computed result with a literal list, not a literal with a literal), row 11 (B4a prefix, B4b suffix), row 9 (B4c reverts only the remainder rule, leaving the sweep intact, and the premise fails).
- Id guard: rows 1, 2, 3, 7 (B5a deletes the dot test, B5b the `%3F` test, B5c the second decode, B5d/B5e skip the result in each of the two routes); row 11 (raw sockets).
- A1: rows 1 and 11 (A1a keeps the 401-shaped answer and drops only the clearing; A1b goes back to `useSession`, the form the old test accepted). A2: rows 1 and 12 (the 504 branch is a separate path; arms A2b, A2e). A5: rows 2 and 3 (a range; a sibling exact version).

**[CORRECTED IN PLACE in "Stage 1 — fix round 2" below: at the time of fix round 1 the check was load-bearing for the reviewer's `R2_B2b_check_deleted` arm (3 of 3 leaked without it); "unarmed" below describes only the state after N1's line.]** **B2b survived, and that is a finding about my own fix, not about the guard.** With the listener attached early, deleting the post-await `res.destroyed || signal.aborted` check changes nothing observable: the early listener aborts the signal, the fetch rejects, and the catch returns. The check stays as defense in depth for the window in which `res.destroyed` is set but `close` has not been emitted yet (Node emits `close` on a later tick), which no test can drive reliably; it is unarmed, and it is the leader's call whether to delete it (I kept it because it costs three lines and the review asked for it by name). The arm that proves the claim is B2a (listener after the await AND check deleted: the original defect), in-process and on the production build.

**Corrections to the "Stage 1" section above (marked here, the earlier text is unchanged):**

CORRECTED: the gate is on every path and a JSON content type is required.
- Section 8, B19 ("the JWT is in no log line"): the guard saw strings only. CORRECTED (reviewer B3): objects are rendered at unlimited depth, and the production output is asserted.
- Section 5, SSE row "client closes -> upstream aborted" and B09: the claim held only after the upstream's headers had arrived. CORRECTED (reviewer B2).
- Section 8, B05 (`sessionHeader: false`): after A1 `readSession` unseals the cookie itself, so the `x-<name>-session` header is not read by any read path and deleting `sessionHeader: false` is now an EQUIVALENT mutant (the reviewer's arm `R_B05` re-run on the new code: `failed 0, passed 1` of 35). - Section 5 "typed" etc.: unchanged. Section 8 total "62 arms" is unchanged; this round adds the 32 above.

## Stage 1 — fix round 2

Scope: N1, the B2b record correction and B05 from `progress/review_web_app.md` "Stage 1 re-review (fix round 1)". Nothing else touched.

### The change

- `apps/web/src/server/routes/api/orders/stream.get.ts:22-26`: right after `res.on('close', onClientGone)`, `if (res.destroyed) { res.off('close', onClientGone); return undefined; }`. The client that left during `readSession` had its `close` emitted before the listener existed, so the upstream was opened for nobody.
- New contract case in `apps/web/src/server/testing/bff-contract.ts`: `R-stream: a client that disconnects in the same tick it sends the request opens no upstream connection` (3 raw sockets write the request and `destroy()` at once, against a fake Gateway that never sends headers; after 1500 ms `gateway.open.size` must be 0 and no `/orders/stream` request may have arrived). Runs in-process and on the production build (the contract is shared). Import `connect` from `node:net` added.
- `R-session: h3's default session header ...` extended with a cookie-less request carrying only `x-otc_session-session`.

| N1_prod | same, production build rebuilt from the mutated source | production `same tick` | AssertionError: the upstream connection stayed open for a client that had already left: expected 3 to be +0 // Object.is equality | c1b8cbdf247e | identical |

### 3. CLDR measurement (script `apps/web/scripts/cldr-disagreement.mjs`, re-run this session, output byte-identical to the earlier one: `.arm/web29s2/cldr.rerun.out`)

Node v24.19.0, ICU 78.3, CLDR 48.0. The table has 26 rows; CLDR disagrees on **1** of them (IQD: ISO 3, CLDR 0). Over `Intl.supportedValuesOf('currency')` (162 codes) with the table's default of 2 elsewhere, CLDR disagrees on **17** (AFN, ALL, COP, HUF, IDR, IQD, IRR, KPW, LAK, LBP, MGA, MMK, PKR, SLL, SOS, SYP, YER). Angular's `getNumberOfCurrencyDigits` disagrees on **14** (AFN, ALL, IQD, IRR, KPW, LAK, LBP, MGA, MMK, RSD, SLL, SOS, SYP, YER). HUF, an assertion currency, is therefore ISO 2 against CLDR 0.

### 4. ids 100 and 102 search (scripts `.arm/web29s2/ids100_102.py`, `ids100_102_all.py`; re-run, outputs identical: `.arm/web29s2/ids100_102*.rerun.out`)

(a) Timeline summaries (`services/projector/src/otc_projector/application/summaries.py`): the f-strings are lines 46, 50, 57, 69, 78, 84, 91, 99, 103, 107, 111, 124; only 78 (`credit_approved`) and 84 (`credit_rejected`) interpolate an amount, both through `format_money`. Whole-string assertions: `test_summaries.py:77` ("Credit hold of 92.45 EUR approved"), `:81` ("Credit hold of 5 000 JPY rejected (simulated_cents_rule)"), `:173-177` (the exponent table by integer arithmetic). Invoice and payment summaries carry references only.

(b) Command: `python3 .arm/web29s2/ids100_102_all.py` (every f-string that is the argument of a `raise` or of `super().__init__` in `services/*/src` and `packages/*/src`: **226** hits; 35 with a money-like name). Classification of the 35 (full output in `.arm/web29s2/ids100_102.out`):
- through `format_money` (8): `orders place_order.py:105`, `orders domain/errors.py:26`, `billing domain/errors.py:18,32,47,110`, `billing invoice_errors.py:39,97`.
- names a currency code or a reference or a status, no amount (14): `billing application/errors.py:29,41,56,71`, `billing domain/errors.py:81`, `invoice_errors.py:27,87,111` (`received`/`expected` are currency codes, e.g. "A payment in 'USD' cannot settle an invoice in 'EUR'."), `buyer_credit.py:160`, `gateway errors.py:68,78`, `command_payloads.py:103`, `payment_register.py:174`, `shared_kernel gln.py:28`.
- raw minor units, but unreachable from a problem document (snapshot-corruption invariants raised as `InvalidBuyerCreditSnapshotError` / `InvalidInvoiceSnapshotError`; `credit_rpc_errors.py` maps only the request errors to RPC codes, and these fall to the internal error): `billing buyer_credit.py:144,148,153` (153 formats a `Money` with `__str__`, "12345 EUR" in minor units, `money.py:112`), `billing invoice.py:264,269` (5 hits in all).
- not money (units, indexes, limits of other kinds): `fulfillment domain/errors.py:17` (unit counts), `projector read_model_indexes.py:87,105,118` (index names), `seed composition.py:27`, `seed sagas.py:670` (a seed-time `ValueError`, never served), `gateway login_throttle.py:32` (rate limit) (7).
- **one observation for the leader (not fixed, `services/**` out of bounds):** `services/billing/src/otc_billing/presentation/invoice_wire.py:74` raises `InvalidInvoiceRequestError(f"the sum of unitPrice x units is above {MAX_MINOR_UNITS} (a 64-bit column)")`, which `credit_rpc_errors.py:63` returns as `validation_failed` with `str(error)`, so the number 9223372036854775807 can reach a problem `detail`. It names a platform limit in minor units, not an amount of a given currency and there is no currency at the site; I classify it as not an id 100/102 defect, but the leader should decide whether a limit counts as "an amount".

Count check, by the listed hits: 8 (format_money) + 14 (codes, references, statuses) + 5 (raw minor units, unreachable) + 7 (not money) + 1 (`invoice_wire.py:74`, the observation) = **35**, the number the script prints. The line-by-line listing is `.arm/web29s2/ids100_102.out`.

I started `postgres mongodb kafka nats` (waited for Kafka healthy), Orders 8101, Fulfillment 8102, Billing 8103, Projector 8105, the Gateway 3001 (all `/health/ready` 200) and `serve.mjs preview` on 3010.

- Signed in; the list showed 20 rows, "Page 1 of 2 · 24 orders".
- Order A (CarrefourEs, PRD-0001 x 2): running total "499.98 EUR"; the BFF received `{"retailerCode":"CarrefourEs","companyCode":"IBERFOODS","currency":"EUR","lines":[{"productCode":"PRD-0001","quantity":2}]}`; live without reload `placed` -> `stock_reserved` -> `credit_approved` -> `confirmed` -> `despatched` -> `invoiced` in 1.1 s, connection "Live"; **one** stream request for the page view; reload at `invoiced`: same status, same 6 entry ids, "Live" again, one new stream request; payment registered through the Gateway (201, INV-000019, 49998 EUR) and the open page went `paid` -> `completed` with no reload; total shown "499.98 EUR".
- Order B (the `.99` demo, unit price 24999): body `{... "lines":[{"productCode":"PRD-0001","quantity":1,"unitPrice":24999}],"notes":"demo — compensation path (.99)"}`; reached `cancelled` (reason `credit_rejected`) live; reloading the terminal order kept the status and 5 entries.
- "Caused by" audit against backlog 201's rule (`causationAudit`): A at invoiced: 1 link in the DOM (`order.confirmed.v1` -> `credit.approved.v1`), 5 entries with a command cause and no link; `rule_matches_dom: true`, `allLinkTargetsExist: true`, `commandIdsRenderedAnywhere: false`. A completed and B cancelled (link: `order.cancelled.v1` -> `stock.released.v1`) the same.
- List newest first (ORD-000026 cancelled 249.99 EUR, ORD-000025 completed 499.98 EUR); the status filter `cancelled` sent `status=cancelled` and showed "Page 1 of 1 · 6 orders". Console problems: none.
- **Probe limitation, stated honestly:** the script counts established TCP connections from the web process to port 3001 and showed 3 both on the order page and 2.5 s after leaving it (and 3 at the end on the list page, where no stream is open), so those 3 are keep-alive pool connections, not streams; this probe cannot show the stream closing. That property is proven by `R-stream-live` (the page closes its stream) and `bff.spec.ts` / `production-bff.spec.ts` (the BFF aborts its upstream when the client goes), with arm P08.
- The waiting state (202) was not seen live: the projector answered each read before the page needed it (`sawWaitingState:false`). It is proven only by the R55 web-half test and arms P13/P14.

After (`.arm/web29s2/live/after.txt`): I stopped every process I started (waited on the PIDs: none alive) and `docker compose stop postgres mongodb kafka nats`; `docker ps` and `ss -ltnp` match the "before" listing (only `otcpy-n8n` running, as found; its restart-loop noted earlier was a startup artefact and it was healthy when I started).

### Surprises

- The first B2 fix ("origin must be library") failed on TanStack's own gc timer: `origin` was "any frame in src/app", and a query created from a page's `injectQuery` puts the page on the stack. The asker (first frame) is the right unit.
- A `deepEqual` failure does not print its diff through the JSON reporter: the first version failed but named nothing. The message now carries the timers.
- An unparseable number reports `value === ""`, so the page's own refusal text for "not a number" was unreachable for any `type="number"` box until `validity.badInput` was read. #8's text inputs do not have this; #7's `type="number"` boxes do (`place.vue:381,395`), so #7 very likely has the same gap (not probed).

### Item 8 (leader's addition): keystrokes typed while the page loads were rewritten ("19.99" shown as "9919", sent as 991900)

You were right: it is a product defect, not a probe artifact. My earlier "the rerun was clean" and "hydration race" explanations are withdrawn.

**Mechanism, by experiment** (all in `.arm/web29s2f1/live/`):

1. `bare.mjs` (a bare `<input type="number" step="0.01">` in Chromium, no Angular): after the keys `1 9 .`, the screen shows `19.` while `input.value` reports `19`. Assigning `input.value = input.value` (the same string) replaces the screen text with `19` and moves the caret to the start. The next keys `99` then give `9919`. Output: `after 19. { before: '19', after: '19' }` then `then 99 -> 9919`.
2. In the page, `[value]="line.unitPrice"` is an assignment (`R.setProperty`, logged by `hold.mjs`: `SET "1" (was "1")`, `SET "19" (was "19")`, ...). Angular renders AFTER the keystroke (zoneless scheduler, a task later). When a render lands after the `.` key and before the next, it assigns `19` over the on-screen `19.`. That is why it is intermittent: it depends on render lag, and a cold page is slower. 3 of 8 cold starts of the original `bytes.mjs` showed it (first two rows' unit price: `19.99/290, 19.99/0.29, 9919/0.29, 19.99/290, 19.99/0.29, 9919/0.29, 19.99/0.29, 19.99/290`), including `0.29` shown as `290`.
3. Not the cause (excluded by experiment): the catalogue arriving (`HOLD=catalog` and `HOLD=none` behave the same), the session read, the `@for` track identity (the unit-price input is never replaced), a recreated line object (binding unchanged), focus (no blur logged).
4. Deterministic reproduction in the production build (before the fix), `hold.mjs` with `LAG=150 GAP=60`: an init script delays every `setTimeout` and `requestAnimationFrame` callback by 150 ms, so renders trail the keys. `HOLD=none` shown `9919`; `HOLD=catalog` (catalogue held on an unresolved promise, released after the third key) shown `9919`. With `LAG=0` or `LAG=400` (renders before all keys, or after all keys) the text is `19.99`. Output in `live/lag.out`. After the fix the same two commands show `19.99` and `19.99` (`live/lag_after.out`).

**Fix:** a directive `components/draft-value.ts` (`input[appDraftValue]`) replaces `[value]` on the three `type="number"` inputs (quantity, unit price, line discount). It assigns `element.value` only when `element.value !== text`, so the typed text and caret are never touched, and a draft changed from outside (the demo button) is still written. The text fields (retailer, notes, currency, product code) keep `[value]`: their screen text and `value` cannot diverge.

**Guard:** `place-order.spec.ts` › `R-typing (stage 2 fix round 1, item 8)`:
- `it.each` over Quantity, Unit price override and Line discount: the three catalogue reads wait on one unresolved promise (asserted in flight: 3 started), the field is typed character by character (`1`, `19`, `19.9`, `19.99`), the catalogue is released after the second key, renders interleave (30 ms between keys), the field text equals the typed text after every key and after the release, and after choosing retailer/company/product and placing the order the body contains `"quantity":25`, `"unitPrice":1999`, `"lineDiscount":29` respectively.
- The same test records every assignment to `input.value` that stores the text the input already holds (`redundantWrites`) and requires none.
- `a draft changed from OUTSIDE the input (the demo button) is still written into it`.

**What jsdom can and cannot show:** jsdom has no divergence between screen text and `value`, so it cannot show the caret move. The guard therefore asserts the CAUSE (an assignment of the held text) plus the text and the bytes. The effect itself is guarded by the Chromium commands above, which are not in `test:ci` (they need the production build and a browser); I did not add a Chromium test to the `src/build/` harness because that harness has no browser dependency and adding one is a dependency decision for the leader.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| G02 | the same on unit price | `R-typing` | `...: expected [ '1', '19', '19.9', '19.99' ] to deeply equal []` | 230593fdeca7 |

| G05 | directive: never assign | the demo-button test | `AssertionError: expected '' to be '249.99'` | 5bd0b086180b |

## Stage 3 of 4: stock and billing pages (full group, `sdd: false`)

Brief `progress/brief_impl_web_app_stage3.md`, premise check `progress/premise_impl_web_app_stage3.md` (15 verified, 0 false, 2 unverifiable). Feature 29 stays `in_progress`; I did not touch `feature_list.json`. Everything is under `apps/web/**`; backups, drivers, logs and live output are in the git-ignored `.arm/web29s3/`. **Process classification: full** (Register payment parses money).

### 1. What was built

| Area | Files (all under `apps/web/src`) |
|---|---|
| Stock page: list, filters, pager, names (id 101), replenish panel | `app/pages/(app)/stock/index.page.ts` (new) |
| Billing page: credits, invoices, filters, pager x2, Register payment panel | `app/pages/(app)/billing/index.page.ts` (new) |
| Navigation: Stock and Billing links in the shell | `app/pages/(app).page.ts` |
| Pager landmark name (`label` input, default `Pagination`; billing names its two) | `app/components/pager.ts` |
| Quantity box brought under the number-box rule (`typeQuantity`, `unparseable.quantity`, a distinct sentence for an unreadable text) | `app/pages/(app)/orders/place.page.ts` |
| Fixtures, answers, helpers | `app/testing/stock-billing-fixtures.ts` (new), `app/testing/number-input.ts` (new: `typeUnparseable`, `typeParseable`, `writesWhileTyping`), `app/testing/page-sweep.ts` (six success answers) |
| Tests | `app/stock.spec.ts` (39), `app/billing.spec.ts` (55), `app/number-boxes.spec.ts` (23), `server/money-bytes.spec.ts` (7), all new; `app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` (+54), `app/place-order.spec.ts` (rename, cause-level guard, one message), `app/session.spec.ts` (one line) |

Behaviour, by what the user sees:

- **Stock** (`/stock`): a table of one row per (company, product): company, product (name, then the code in brackets), on hand, reserved, available (badge flagged when `available < threshold`), threshold, and a **Replenish** button. Filters: Company, Product (text, committed on change), **Low stock only** (`belowThreshold`, sent only when on). 20 per page. States are distinct: loading (only "Loading stock…"), empty, error (the problem's own text), table. The pager claims no count while loading or after a failed read. Replenish opens one panel above the table; its **Units to add** box is a `type="number"` box under the place-order pattern; the body is exactly `ReplenishStockRequest` (`{"companyCode":…,"lines":[{"productCode":…,"units":<integer>}]}`); a synchronous `submitting` signal makes a second submit in the same task a no-op (a top-up is a delta, so a repeat adds again); success shows "Added N units to CODE. On hand is now M." (or says the answer did not include the line) and re-reads the list; 4xx and 5xx show the problem's own text. The form says it adds, is not idempotent, and that after a timeout the list should be read again.
- **Billing** (`/billing`): **Credit limits** (filters Credit retailer, Credit company; table of limit, held, open exposure, available; an exhausted line flagged) and **Invoices** (filters Invoice status, retailer, company, order reference, and **Issued at least (minutes) ago**, a number box; table; **Register payment** only on `issued` invoices). Money is `displayMoney` from the ISO 4217 table, never `Intl`. Each list has its own pager (`Credits pagination`, `Invoices pagination`) and its own loading, empty and error states, independent of the other. **Register payment**: one panel; the **Amount** box is a number box whose `step` follows the *invoice's* currency exponent and is prefilled with the invoice's exact total in its own decimals; **Payment reference** is prefilled `PAY-<UTC date>-<invoice number>` (at most 30 characters, the wire limit; it IS the idempotency key, so there is no header); the body is exactly `RegisterPaymentRequest` (`paymentReference`, `amount: {amount, currency}`, `valueDate` as `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.mmmZ`, `source: "operator"`), sent once; `201 accepted` renders "recorded (HTTP 201). Invoice X is now paid.", `200 duplicate` renders a different amber sentence ("already recorded (HTTP 200). Nothing new was created: this was an idempotent replay."); both lists re-read on success (the payment releases credit); 400, 404, 409 (both codes), 422, 503, 500 show the problem's own text; the panel survives the paid invoice leaving an `issued` list.
- **Number boxes** (item 4): a population guard (section 4).
- **Item 8** (navigation): the shell links to both pages and both links are in the sweep's control population. I did **not** add a link from the order detail page to billing: the invoice list already filters by order reference, and a link would be one more control and one more route to review in stage 4 without an acceptance item behind it (brief: "may").

### 4. Number-box population

Command (a search result, not a sample; the population is `src/app`, non-test sources, `type="number"` in a template):

```
$ grep -rnoE '<input[^>]*type="number"[^>]*data-testid="[a-z-]+"|<input[^>]*data-testid="[a-z-]+"[^>]*type="number"' src/app --include=*.ts --exclude='*.spec.ts' | sed -E 's/(<input).*data-testid="([a-z-]+)".*/\1 ... data-testid="\2"/'
src/app/pages/(app)/orders/place.page.ts:146:<input ... data-testid="quantity-input"
src/app/pages/(app)/orders/place.page.ts:153:<input ... data-testid="unit-price-input"
src/app/pages/(app)/orders/place.page.ts:160:<input ... data-testid="line-discount-input"
src/app/pages/(app)/stock/index.page.ts:93:<input ... data-testid="replenish-units-input"
src/app/pages/(app)/billing/index.page.ts:139:<input ... data-testid="invoice-age-input"
src/app/pages/(app)/billing/index.page.ts:160:<input ... data-testid="payment-amount-input"
$ grep -rlE 'type="number"' src/app --include=*.ts --exclude='*.spec.ts' | sort
src/app/components/draft-value.ts          (a comment)
src/app/pages/(app)/billing/index.page.ts
src/app/pages/(app)/orders/place.page.ts
src/app/pages/(app)/stock/index.page.ts
src/app/testing/number-input.ts            (a comment)
```

Six members: three existing (`quantity-input`, `unit-price-input`, `line-discount-input`) and three new (`replenish-units-input`, `invoice-age-input`, `payment-amount-input`). The guard (`number-boxes.spec.ts`) does not use this grep: it reads each component's `template` with the TypeScript parser (a comment is not a template), finds inputs with an HTML parser (an HTML comment is not an input), compares the members to a **literal** expected set both ways (a new or a vanished box fails by name), and requires of every member `[appDraftValue]`, no `[value]`, and an `(input)` handler that is a method of the component whose body has a real `badInput` property read (a comment or a string saying "badInput" does not count). The analyser is armed first by 14 sentinels that must be found and one clean case that must not be (`describe('the analyser is armed…')`).

**What the guard cannot see, and what covers it** (defeat-list rows 4, 5, 6, 11, 12; the header of `number-boxes.spec.ts` carries the same text):

- **Row 4** (shadowed in a comment or a string): *seen*, not merely survived: an HTML comment, a TypeScript comment and a `"…badInput…"` string in a handler each have a sentinel test. Negative control N7 (an input inside an HTML comment in the stock template) correctly **passes**.
- **Row 5** (dead region): a number box inside `@if (false)` IS counted (stricter); a handler whose `badInput` read sits in `if (false)` **passes the guard** (arm N4a SURVIVED, by design). The behaviour tests see it (arm N4b KILLED).
- **Row 6** (raw or triple-quoted string): `String.raw`, plain and no-substitution templates are read (sentinel); a template that is a variable or has an interpolation is **reported as unreadable and fails**, never skipped.
- **Row 11** (a form the instrument does not recognise): `templateUrl`, an `.html` file, `[type]`/`[attr.type]`/interpolated `type`, and `createElement('input')` in code each fail by name (sentinels). An input rendered by a third-party component is beyond it.
- **Row 12** (a path the population never drives): a box outside `src/app` is outside the population. Also, **which draft a handler writes** is invisible: arm N5a (the payment box's `(input)` calls `typeAge`, which does read `badInput`) SURVIVED the guard; the behaviour test sees it (N5b KILLED).

### 6. Arming table

Protocol: `cp` backup into `.arm/web29s3/<id>/bak_*` with `sha256` recorded, ONE mutation, ONE named test (`vitest run <file> -t <name>`) under `setsid --wait timeout -k 10`, verdict from the JSON artifact (`--reporter=json --outputFile`), restore by `cp` from the backup, `cmp`/`sha256` identical, `.vite` and `.angular` cleared; a tracked path is never deleted or moved (a new page file for W9 is created and removed). Driver `.arm/web29s3/arm.py`, definitions `.arm/web29s3/defs.py`, every run `.arm/web29s3/results.jsonl` (a later run of an id replaces the row below; `arms_rerun*.log` hold the re-runs). **83 distinct arms**: 79 KILLED (the failure names the claim), 4 SURVIVED, all four expected and classified below. The `sha256` column is the first 12 hex of the original file (`cmp`: restored file identical). The first failure message is verbatim (truncated at 230 characters; where the reporter abbreviates a diff, the failing test's own title, shown first in backticks, names the claim).

Classification of the survivors (a survivor is a finding unless classified):

- `N4a_dead_region_guard`: the guard cannot see a `badInput` read in dead code (defeat row 5). **Accepted by design and covered by behaviour**: `N4b` (same mutation, behaviour test) KILLED.
- `N5a_sibling_handler_guard`: the guard cannot see which draft a handler writes (row 3). **Accepted by design**: `N5b` KILLED.
- `N7_html_comment_negative_control`: expected to PASS (an input in an HTML comment is not a member). It is the negative control that shows the analyser reads templates with a parser and not with a pattern.
- `H01_directive_writes_once` against the renamed place-order test: SURVIVED, as the stage 2 re-review said; the rename makes the test say it proves creation; `S13b` and `B18b` KILLED the same mutation.

Four arms were SURVIVED on their first run and are the finding in section 10, item 2 (equivalent mutants): `N3b`, `N3d`, `N4b`, `N8b`. They are KILLED in the table because the code now gives an unreadable text its own sentence; the first-run rows are kept in `.arm/web29s3/results_batch1.jsonl`.

### 7. Defeat-list rows applied

Applied to the three things I built, and which rows do not apply (reason):

| Row | Number-box guard | Stock and billing tests | Sweep extension |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 delete the behaviour | N3a, N8, N9 (guard); N3b/N3d/N4b/N8b/N9b (behaviour); N2a/N2b | S1, S4, S8, S9, S11, B8, B9, B16, B20 | W1/W2 (error text replaced), W8 (catalogue request dropped) |

| 4 shadow in a comment or string | sentinel tests; N7 | n/a (behavioural tests do not read source) | n/a |
| 5 hide in a dead region | N4a survives by design, N4b kills | n/a | n/a |
| 6 raw / triple-quoted string | sentinel `String.raw`, plain, no-substitution; unreadable template fails | n/a | n/a |
| 7 drop an optional element | sentinel: no `data-testid`; N2a/N2b drop `[appDraftValue]` | S3 (`productName`, the optional field) | n/a |
| 8 compare a literal to a literal | the expected set is a literal and the observed set is derived by the parser | fixtures: no name contains a code and no code a name (S2), distinct amounts per table | the reviewed tables are literals compared to observed runs |
| 9 satisfy the closer half, leave the premise stale | N1 (a new box fails by name both ways) | W8: the catalogue request left the load population and `EXPECTED_LOAD` failed | W6 (stale label), W5 (late timer), W3 (a new consumer): all KILLED |
| 10 build output or caches join the population | the walk is `src/app` only and excludes `testing/` (asserted); `dist/` and `.angular/` are outside it | n/a | the page walk is `src/app/pages` (stage 1) |

### 8. Live record (`.arm/web29s3/live/live.mjs`, output `live.out`; real Chromium (Playwright core from #7's `node_modules`, read only) against the PRODUCTION build on 3010 and the REAL Gateway, Orders, Fulfillment, Billing and Projector hosts over the developer stack; nothing mocked)

Setup: `docker ps` before = `otcpy-n8n` only; `ss -ltn` snapshot before saved. Started `postgres mongodb kafka nats`, waited for Kafka healthy, started the four hosts (8101, 8102, 8103, 8105), the Gateway (3001) and `scripts/serve.mjs preview` (3010) after `pnpm run build`. I did not touch 3017 or anything in `../order-to-cash-nestjs` except importing Playwright from its `node_modules`.

- **First run, a finding** (`live_run1.out`): `getByRole('button', {name: 'Replenish PRD-0001'})` resolved to **two** elements. The developer data has **215 stock lines** across several companies (`ALBIONFOODS`, `BAUWERK`, …), so the same product is on many rows and my buttons' accessible names were ambiguous. No unit fixture had two companies. **Fixed here** (not deferred): the name is `Replenish <code> for <company>`; new tests `R-replenish-company` (two rows of one product, distinct names, the row's company is sent, the outcome reports THIS row's level and not the other company's line of the same product); arms S19, S20, S21 KILLED; the sweep's `CONTROLS` follow.
- **Names (id 101)** on the live list: `Ration Pack Bundle (PRD-0001)`, `Pasta 500g Case (24u) (PRD-0002)`, `Olive Oil 1L Case (12u) (PRD-0003)` ... and one catalogue request (`/api/catalog/products`). Pager `Page 1 of 11 · 215 stock lines`.
- **Replenish** in the real number box: typing `1-2` gives `value ""` and `validity.badInput true` (Chromium; the jsdom tests simulate this); the page said "Units to add: the browser could not read what was typed. Enter a whole number of at least 1." and sent nothing. Typing `7` sent the bytes `{"companyCode":"ALBIONFOODS","lines":[{"productCode":"PRD-0001","units":7}]}`, answered `200`; the outcome read "Added 7 units to PRD-0001. On hand is now 514." and the row changed `507 -> 514` (the list re-read).
- **Order** placed through the UI with a unit price override of `0.29` (bytes `…"quantity":1,"unitPrice":29}`), reached `invoiced` (ORD-000028), invoice `INV-000021` found with the filter `Invoice order reference` (list and pager: `Page 1 of 1 · 1 invoices`), total `0.29 EUR`, status `issued`.
- **Register payment**: the box was prefilled `0.29` with `step 0.01`; typing `1-2` gave `badInput true`, the page said "Amount: the browser could not read what was typed. Enter an amount with at most 2 decimals (EUR)." and sent nothing; typing `0.29` key by key gave `value "0.29"`, `badInput false`, and the browser sent **`{"paymentReference":"PAY-2026-10-15-000021","amount":{"amount":29,"currency":"EUR"},"valueDate":"2026-10-15T15:28:28.131Z","source":"operator"}`** to `/api/invoices/<id>/payments`, answered `201`; the page read "Payment PAY-2026-10-15-000021 recorded (HTTP 201). Invoice INV-000021 is now paid."
- **The same remittance again**: the same `paymentReference`, answered `200` with `Idempotent-Replay: true`; the page read "Payment PAY-2026-10-15-000021 was already recorded (HTTP 200). Nothing new was created: this was an idempotent replay."
- The invoice list re-read to `paid`; the order reached **`completed`**; **credits** listed (`Page 1 of 8 · 154 credit lines`), e.g. `AldiDe / ALBIONFOODS: 5 000.00 EUR limit, 0.00 held, 0.00 exposure, 5 000.00 available`; screenshot `live/billing.png`.
- Not done live: BHD `1.005` and JPY `1999` end to end (I did not probe whether the developer data holds a BHD or JPY retailer); both are proved at the seam by `R-payment-bytes` and at the BFF by `R-bytes (BFF)`. The second run repeated the whole script after fixing my probe's invoice-reference regular expression (the first run stopped there, after the replenish and one order were already done: `live_run1.out`), so the stack holds two replenishments of 7 and two orders (ORD-000027 invoiced and left unpaid, ORD-000028 completed).
- Teardown: the web server by process group; the four hosts' `uvicorn` listeners and the Gateway by PID (after the broker was stopped their shutdown hung, so they were killed by PID, listed); `docker compose stop postgres mongodb kafka nats`; `docker ps` shows only `otcpy-n8n` (as found); `ss -ltn` local addresses identical to the snapshot before.

### 9. Gates (final tree; logs `.arm/web29s3/logs/gate_*.log`, `gates.txt`, script `gates.sh`, each under `setsid --wait timeout -k 10`; run after the last source edit, and every arm was re-run on that tree afterwards)

- `pnpm@12.8.1 install --frozen-lockfile`: exit 0; `run --if-present lint`: exit 0 (no script); `run types:check`: exit 0; `run build`: exit 0.
- `run test:ci`: exit 0, **Test Files 32 passed (32), Tests 644 passed (644)**. Reconciliation with the brief's 28 / 466: **466 + 39 (`stock.spec.ts`) + 55 (`billing.spec.ts`) + 23 (`number-boxes.spec.ts`) + 54 (sweep: 2 pages x 3, 16 actions x 3) + 7 (`server/money-bytes.spec.ts`) = 644**, and 28 + 4 new files = 32. (An earlier full run, before the BFF bytes file existed, read 31 files / 637 tests; its one failure, `session.spec.ts` expecting only `/orders` in the navigation, was the stale line I then updated.)
- `uv run pytest packages/shared_kernel/tests/test_currency_exponent.py`: 15 passed.
- Final `git status --short` differs from the snapshot taken at the start only by my new files; arming restored every file (`cmp` identical).

### 10. Findings, surprises, not done

1. **Found live, fixed:** replenish buttons were ambiguous when one product is held by several companies (section 8).
2. **Equivalent mutants, fixed:** on the three boxes where an empty text is refused anyway (quantity, replenish units, payment amount) deleting the `badInput` read was unobservable: `N3b`, `N3d`, `N4b`, `N8b` SURVIVED on the first run, so the "both properties" rule had a property no behaviour test could prove. The fix gives an unreadable text its own sentence ("…the browser could not read what was typed…"), which is also the right words for a person who typed `1-2`. This **changes an existing line** (place-order quantity: an unreadable text now reads "Quantity: the browser could not read what was typed. Enter a whole number of at least 1."; an empty one keeps the old sentence); the test line was updated and arm `N3d` KILLED it.
3. **#7 (not touched, for the leader):** `apps/web/app/pages/stock/index.vue:97-98` accepts fractional units (`Number.isFinite(delta) && delta > 0`) and silently does nothing on bad input; its replenish outcome still has the `?? '…'` ellipsis (`:277`); its pager claims `data?.page.total ?? 0` while loading (`:292`). Same class as the stage 2 note about #7's number boxes.
4. **Not ported, open for the maintainer:** #8's order-link lookup after a payment (an extra request), and a link from the order detail page to billing (the brief says "may").
5. **`specs/shared/test-matrix.md` not updated:** `specs/**` is outside this brief's bounds; the test names in section 1 are the rows to enter.
6. **Sweep specifics:** the label `PRD-0001` in `LABELS` is fixture-specific (the code-only row of a product the failed catalogue cannot name); it goes stale, and says so, if the fixture changes. The premise "every reviewed label is produced" cannot be armed with `-t` alone (it needs the memoised runs of the whole file and times out at 480 s alone), so arm W6 runs the whole file and reads that test's verdict. The first arm of B10 was a compile error, not a behavioural kill; it was redefined (`?? 0`) and re-run.
7. **Interruption:** the session was stopped when the maintainer's VS Code closed (about 17:46). On resume I verified the tree (every arm restored, `cmp` identical, no host or container left running), re-ran the gates on the final tree, **re-ran 60 arms** that predated later code edits (the aria-label, the unreadable-text sentences, the typing counter), and finished this record. First-pass rows are in `.arm/web29s3/results_before_rerun_all.jsonl`.
8. No backend defect found. No finding is rooted in `specs/shared/`.

### Effort and packages

Effort: **119 minutes** wall-clock from the recorded start (`.arm/web29s3/start_epoch`) to the record, before the resume re-runs; the interruption and the 60-arm re-run (about 10 minutes) come on top and I did not time them separately. Comparison with #7's and #8's baselines is the leader's to record in `progress/history.md`.

Packages installed: none.
