### Item 1: search of every filter and box for a dependence on `change` (command and output; one classification per hit)

```
$ grep -rnoE '<(input|select)[^>]*\(change\)="[^"]*"' src/app --include=*.ts --exclude='*.spec.ts'     (16 hits)
orders/index.page.ts:42,51,63  <select> status, retailer, company          -> select: always holds a readable value; `change` fires on choice. OK
orders/index.page.ts:75        <input> reference (text)                     -> text has no unreadable state; `change` on blur/Enter commits it. OK
stock/index.page.ts:67,71      <input> company, product (text)              -> same as above. OK
stock/index.page.ts:74         <input type=checkbox> low stock only         -> `change` fires on click. OK
billing/index.page.ts:69,73    <input> credit retailer, company (text)      -> text. OK
billing/index.page.ts:118      <select> invoice status                      -> select. OK
billing/index.page.ts:127,131,135 <input> invoice retailer, company, order  -> text. OK
billing/index.page.ts:139      <input type=number> age                      -> THE number box that depended on `change`: FIXED (refuses on `input`)
orders/place.page.ts:90,103,134 <select> retailer, company, product         -> select. OK
```
The other five number boxes (quantity, unit price, line discount, replenish units, payment amount) commit by `input` and by submit, never by `change`; each has a behaviour test that fires only `input` (`typeUnparseable`).

**Probe, production build, real Chromium, stub Gateway (`.arm/web29s3f1/live/age.mjs`, `age.out`; the reviewer's script reused unchanged):**

```
{"typed":"1-2","commit":"Tab","box":{"value":"","badInput":true},"changeEvents":0,"reads":[],"problem":"Issued at least (minutes) ago: enter a whole number of minutes, 0 or more."}
{"typed":"1-2","commit":"Enter","box":{"value":"","badInput":true},"changeEvents":0,"reads":[],"problem":"Issued at least (minutes) ago: enter a whole number of minutes, 0 or more."}
{"typed":"12e","commit":"Tab","box":{"value":"","badInput":true},"changeEvents":0,"reads":[],"problem":"Issued at least (minutes) ago: enter a whole number of minutes, 0 or more."}
{"typed":"1.5","commit":"Tab","box":{"value":"1.5","badInput":false},"changeEvents":1,"reads":[],"problem":"Issued at least (minutes) ago: enter a whole number of minutes, 0 or more."}
```
All four rows show the problem (before: three of them showed none).

**`probe.mjs` on the same build** (`live/probe.out`): payments through the BFF to the stub: EUR `0.29` -> `"amount":29` and `19.99` -> `1999` (prefill `1242.50`); BHD `1.005` -> `1005` (prefill `2.005`, step `0.001`), BHD `0.29` -> `290`; JPY `1999` -> `1999` (prefill `5000`, step `1`); JPY `19.99`, EUR `1e3`, `-1`, `1.005` refused in words, nothing sent; `1-2` and `12e` give the unreadable sentence; **`90071992547409.92` now gives "Amount: enter an amount of at most 90071992547409.91 (EUR)."** (A2); MAX_SAFE sends `9007199254740991`; the same remittance twice renders 201 then 200. Replenish: `1.5`, `1e3`, `0`, `9007199254740993` refused, `1-2` unreadable sentence, `7` sends `{"companyCode":"BAUWERK","lines":[{"productCode":"PRD-0001","units":7}]}`. **A1 in Chromium:** `1-2` typed under IBERFOODS' row, then Replenish for BAUWERK: `typedBox {value:"", badInput:true}`, `movedBox {value:"", badInput:false}` (the text is cleared), the heading names `(BAUWERK)`, submitting says the plain sentence and sends nothing. Teardown: web by process group, stub by PID; `docker ps` = `otcpy-n8n` only; `ss -ltn` identical before and after.

### B-S3-2: what each half covers now (the record's rows 6 and 11 for the number-box guard, narrowed)

- **Row 6** (raw or triple-quoted string): the syntax half reads `String.raw`, plain and no-substitution templates; a variable or interpolated template is reported. It does **not** read HTML its parser misreads (R6: `@if (a<b) { <input type="number"> }` is swallowed as a tag); the **render half** covers that for anything a page renders.
- **Row 11** (a form the instrument does not recognise): the syntax half reports `[type]`, `[attr.type]`, `type="{"`, `templateUrl`, `.html` and `createElement('input')` by name. It does **not** recognise `bind-type` (R7) or other Angular spellings; the render half covers them for rendered boxes. (The earlier claim that "a bound type fails by name" was true of three spellings only.)
- **Rows 4, 5, 12** unchanged from section 4 of the Stage 3 record, plus: the render half sees only boxes the sweep's pages and scripted actions put on screen, and it cannot see an unrendered component. The syntax half covers an unrendered component; the render half covers a template the syntax half misreads. A box that is both in a state nobody drives AND in a template the HTML parser misreads is found by neither: stated, not claimed away.
- **R5** is killed by the syntax half (decorator by shape), **not** the render half: a component no page renders has no DOM. The brief asked for R5-R7 on the render half; R5 cannot be, by construction, and is reported as such. R6 and R7 are killed by the render half; R8 by the syntax half (a second writer) and by the cause-level counter (R8b).

Render-half premises (each armed with a sentinel; changing an instrument swaps its premises):

| Premise | Where asserted | Sentinel arm |
|---|---|---|
| every swept page has a baseline run | the render test, first assertion | (population: `pages` is the filesystem, stage 1) |
| every run mounted (a page that crashes shows no boxes, so "no unreviewed box" would be vacuous) | the render test, second assertion | `RH_P1_page_does_not_mount` KILLED (`a state that did not mount: its boxes were never looked at: expected [ '/stock', …(6) ] to deeply equal []`) |
| every form is opened (the units and amount boxes exist only in panels a script opens) | the render test, "Reviewed but rendered in no state the sweep drives" | `RH2` (payment script stops opening) KILLED; `RH1b` (both replenish scripts stop opening) KILLED; `RH1` (only one of the two) **SURVIVED**: the form is opened by two scripts, so removing one is equivalent (classified) |
| **its limit**: a box reachable only through a state no script drives | not claimed | `RH3` **SURVIVED** the render half and `RH3b` KILLED it on the syntax half: the armed demonstration |

## Stage 3 — round 3 (light)

Light, test-only round chosen by the maintainer after the second rejection (re-review: `progress/review_web_app.md` "Stage 3 re-review (fix round 1)"). **No product code changed.** Non-spec files touched: none (`git status --short` equals the snapshot at the start). Only `apps/web/src/app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` and `apps/web/src/app/number-boxes.spec.ts`. Artifacts: `.arm/web29s3f2/` (`arm.py`, `defs.py` which loads the reviewer's `.arm/web29r4/s3r1/defs.py`, `results.jsonl`, `gates.sh`, `logs/`). Effort: **61 minutes** wall-clock (arms of the render half cost about 9 minutes each, because the test now reads every failing run). Packages installed: none.

### 1. Blocking: the render half reads the failure states too

- `Outcome` (the memoised result of a failing run) now keeps `boxes`, the `data-testid` of every number box that run rendered at any moment; a new `failureSweep()` walks exactly the failing runs the label machinery already walks (every key of every page and action, every declared status, both variants) and returns the labels AND the boxes; `labelsProduced()` is now `(await failureSweep()).labels`, so nothing runs twice inside the whole file.
- The render test unions the baselines' boxes with the failing runs' boxes and compares to the literal set both ways; a new premise asserts that failing runs were actually read (`no failing run was read: the failure states were never looked at`). Its timeout is `SWEEP_TIMEOUT * 4` because alone (`-t`) it pays for the whole failure sweep (about 9 minutes), as the label premise does. The message now says "Rendered (in a baseline state or in any failing run) but not reviewed".
- The header of `number-boxes.spec.ts` now says "each page's load, each scripted action, AND every failing run: the DOM of each failed request", which is what the test does.

### 2. X3: narrowed, not resolved (decision and why)

I **narrowed** the header's coverage sentence and kept X3 as the demonstrated limit. Resolving `@Component(META)` means following a const, an import or a spread: an unbounded static analysis for a case with no user impact (the render half sees such a component as soon as a page renders it, arm X4), and reporting "any decorator with a non-literal argument" would flag unrelated decorators. The header now says that a decorator argument that is not an object literal written in place is not read, that such a component is seen by the render half when rendered, and that an UNRENDERED one with const metadata is the stated residual; the sentence "the syntax half covers those" now ends "only when the decorator carries an object literal".

### 3. The `(change)` search count

The Stage 3 fix round 1 record said 16 hits and listed 17 lines. The command's output has **17** hits: orders 4 (status, retailer, company, reference) + stock 3 (company, product, low stock) + billing 7 (credit retailer, credit company, invoice status, invoice retailer, invoice company, invoice order, age) + place 3 (retailer, company, product) = 17. The classifications stand: one number box (the age box, fixed in round 1), sixteen others that cannot hold unreadable text.

### 1. The order link after a payment

**What each predecessor shows (read from the code, file and line):**

| State | #7 (`pages/billing/index.vue`, `composables/useOrders.ts`) | #8 (`billing-view.tsx`, `hooks/use-orders.ts`) | #9 (`pages/(app)/billing/index.page.ts`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| resolving | `index.vue:443` "resolving the order link…" on `isLoading` (`:165`) | `billing-view.tsx:297-298` `isPending` -> "resolving the order link…" | `:189` `orderLink.isLoading()` -> "Resolving the order link…" |
| found | `:440` `NuxtLink` to `/orders/${linkedOrderId}` | `:299-300` `Link` to `/orders/${data}` | `:191-192` `routerLink` `['/orders', orderId]` |
| asked, no order | **nothing** (the `v-if`/`v-else-if` chain at `:440-443` has no third branch) | `:304-305` "no order was found for X, so there is no timeline to link to." | `:193-194` the same sentence, "No order was found for X, ..." |
| lookup failed | **nothing** (`useQuery`'s error is never read) | `:295-296` `ErrorMessage` prefix "the order link could not be resolved", fallback "the request failed" | `:187-188` "The order link could not be resolved: {problem's own detail or title, else 'the request failed'}" |
| no `orderReference` in the response | query disabled, nothing shown | query disabled, **`isPending` stays true: "resolving..." forever** (by reading: React Query v5 reports `pending` for a disabled query; not run) | query disabled, `isLoading` is false: nothing shown (test below) |

So #9 ports #8's four states, shows the failure in the problem's own words (acceptance item 4), and uses `isLoading` where #8 used `isPending` so that a response without an `orderReference` does not hang on "Resolving".

**Built:** `injectQuery` `orderLink` (`index.page.ts:322-326`), key `['orders','by-reference', <reference>]`, `queryFn` = `api.listOrders({ orderReference, page: 1, pageSize: 1 })` taking `items[0]?.orderId ?? null`, `enabled: Boolean(result()?.response.orderReference)`. `result` is set by the mutation's `onSuccess` alone (201 and the 200 replay) and cleared by `openPayment`/`closePayment`, so the lookup cannot start before a success or after a failure. `page=1&pageSize=1` as #7 and #8 send them (the Gateway filters by equality, `mongo_order_read_model.py:225-226`, so the match is the only row). No BFF route was added (`routes/api/orders/index.get.ts` forwards the query string).

**Tests** (`apps/web/src/app/billing-order-link.spec.ts`, 11 tests, ids `R-order-link`), fixtures: order id `a1b2c3d4-0000-4000-8000-00000000f042`, order reference `ORD-000042`, invoice reference `INV-000027`, a decoy second row with its own id and reference; none contains another.

| Acceptance | Test | Arm (fails by name) |
|---|---|---|
| a. href is `/orders/<id of the matching order>` (201 and 200 replay) | `201 accepted: the link's href is /orders/<...>` and `200 duplicate (an idempotent replay): ...` | m1 (link to the reference), m2 (`items[1]`, another row's id), m3a (no parameter: the unfiltered first order) |
| b. `GET /api/orders?orderReference=ORD-000042&page=1&pageSize=1` exactly, once, reference from the response (a different reference is typed in the filter box) | `the request is GET /api/orders with the PAID invoice's orderReference (from the response, not from the filter box), once, and nothing else filtered` | m3a (parameter dropped), m3b (reads the filter box) |
| c. no lookup before a success, none after a failed payment | `no lookup is sent while the panel is merely open, none after a payment that FAILED, and one only after a success` (counts ANY `GET /api/orders`, because an ungated query sends one with no reference, which a regex on `orderReference=` would miss), `a response that carries no orderReference asks nothing ...` | m4 (`enabled: true`) |
| d. not found, failed, resolving: each its own words | `the lookup ANSWERED and found no order`, `the lookup FAILED with 404` / `... with 403` (problem's detail, decoy title absent), `a failed lookup with no problem document ...` (fallback), `while the lookup is in flight only "Resolving the order link..." shows` (held on a gate) | m5a (not-found branch), m5b (error branch), m5c (resolving branch), m5d (fixed words instead of the problem's) |
| also | `closing the panel and opening another invoice drops the link of the previous payment` | not armed separately (it fails if `result` is not cleared; covered by the existing `openPayment` tests) |

**e. Ported-idiom ledger row.** #7 relied on a bare `useQuery` whose error and empty answer rendered nothing (`useOrders.ts:39-48`, `index.vue:440-443`); #8 supplied the four states with `useOrderIdByReference` (`use-orders.ts:25-31`) and `billing-view.tsx:292-307`, but gated "resolving" on `isPending`, which a disabled query satisfies forever (by reading); in #9 the states are supplied by `orderLink` in `index.page.ts:187-195,322-326`, the gate by `enabled` on `result()`'s reference, the failure text by `describeError` (`lib/problem.ts`), and the guard by `billing-order-link.spec.ts` plus the sweep (section 2). The engine claim "a disabled query is not loading" was probed one way (TanStack Query in this build, test `a response that carries no orderReference ...`); the React Query side of #8 is read, not run.

**Live (final tree, production build on 3010, real Gateway, Orders, Fulfillment, Billing, Projector; `.arm/web29s4/live/live.out`):** an order was placed through the Gateway (`ORD-000029`, id `205f2991-4430-4e9f-9d6c-e868b56187cb`), its invoice `INV-000022` issued, the payment registered in Chromium: `lookups before the payment: []`; after it exactly one `/api/orders?orderReference=ORD-000029&page=1&pageSize=1`; the link's href equalled `/orders/205f2991-...`; clicking it landed on the order page showing `ORD-000029`; no page error.

### 2. Sweep completeness

**Stopping rule (written before starting):** the population is every call site of an `ApiClient` method and every `EventSource`/`fetch(` construct under `apps/web/src/app/**`, excluding `*.spec.ts`, `testing/**` and `ui/**`. Complete when each hit is classified as driven by a sweep run (failed with each status in `failureStatuses`) or not a request, and the census counts reconcile.

Command and output (34 hits, classified below):

```
grep -rnE "\bapi\.[A-Za-z]+\(|\bfetch\(|EventSource\(|this\.request[<(]|this\.send\(" apps/web/src/app --include=*.ts --exclude=*.spec.ts --exclude-dir=testing --exclude-dir=ui
```

- **18 hits in `api/api-client.ts`**: `:15` the `fetch` factory and `:25` the `EventSource` factory (the two primitives, the only places they exist: not requests by themselves), `:59` `send`, `:73-114` the 15 method bodies (`this.request<...>`), `:120` `orderStream`. Not call sites; they are the instrument's seam (`seam.spec.ts` fails any request that bypasses it).
- **16 call sites**, each driven: `orders/catalog.ts:8,11,14` (`catalogProducts/Retailers/Companies`: the loads of `/orders`, `/orders/place`, `/stock`); `pages/(app).page.ts:58` `logout` (`/orders | sign-out`); `pages/login.page.ts:63` `login` (`/login | sign-in`); `orders/[id].page.ts:224` `getOrder` (the order page load, `stream-refused`, `retry-connection`, `check-now`, and the `place-order` action) and `:199` `orderStream` (an `EventSource`, `STREAM_EXCEPTIONS`: refused stream asked through `getOrder`); `orders/index.page.ts:134` `listOrders` (load and the five filters); `stock/index.page.ts:175` `listStock` (load, filters) and `:204` `replenishStock` (`/stock | replenish`); `billing/index.page.ts:259` `listCredits`, `:275` `listInvoices` (loads and the filters), `:308` `registerPayment` and **`:324` `listOrders` (the new lookup: `/billing | register-payment`, key `ORDER_LINK_GET`)**; `orders/place.page.ts:226` `placeOrder` (`/orders/place | place-order`); `session/session.ts:10` `session` (every page load).
- 16 + 18 = 34. Anything unclassified: **none**.

**Census reconciled:** `REQUEST_UNITS` billing is now `{ injectQuery: 3, injectMutation: 1, 'enabled:': 1 }` (the lookup is the third query and the first `enabled:` gate); `EXPECTED_ACTION['/billing | register-payment']` gains `ORDER_LINK_GET`; `CONTROLS['/billing']` gains `a:/orders/:id` (the link, "no request: ... swept as its own page"); `LABELS` gains `The order link could not be resolved`. The sweep failed the lookup with every status `GET /api/orders` declares (400, 500, 502, 504) in both variants (problem `detail` and `title`-only) and requires the sentinel on screen. **Armed:** s1 (error branch deleted) fails 4 + 4 sweep cases by name.

**Gap found by the audit and closed (a guard, not only a table):** `REQUEST_UNITS` counts query constructs, so a second `api.x()` call inside an existing `queryFn`, or a client held under another name, would not move any count. Two tests added to `error-text.sweep.spec.ts`: `every call of an ApiClient method is a reviewed one, and the request it makes is one a page load or scripted action drives` (literal `API_CALL_SITES` by file and method with counts, literal `API_METHOD_REQUESTS`, every method's request must be observed by some baseline run, and the files that name `ApiClient` must equal the files with call sites) and `no fetch( or new EventSource( outside the ApiClient`.

**Defeat list for the new guards:** row 1 (delete the behaviour: s1, m4), row 2 (corrupt a supplied field: s5 changes a request pattern), row 3 (substitute a sibling: m2, m1), row 4 (shadow in a comment/string: the call-site pattern matches `api.x(` inside a comment, so a commented-out call counts as a call; accepted, the failure direction is a false alarm, not a hole), row 5/6 (dead region / raw string: same, a match in dead code is counted, again toward a false alarm), row 7 (drop an optional element: the lookup with `orderReference` absent, test `a response that carries no orderReference ...`), row 8 (literal to literal: `API_CALL_SITES` is compared to a scan of the source, not to itself), row 9 (closer half satisfied, premise stale: s5, a stale request pattern fails by name), row 10 (build output joins the population: `appSources()` walks `src/app`, which has no build output), row 11 (a form the instrument does not recognise: the alias file below), row 12 (served through a path the population never drives: s2). **Sentinels:** s2 a throwaway `this.api.listCredits({})` on the stock page; s3 a throwaway `globalThis.fetch(...)` there; s4 a new file `lib/zz-sentinel.ts` holding `const http = inject(ApiClient); http.listStock({})` (removed, absent); s5 the `listCredits` request pattern corrupted.

### 4. Backlog 201 ("caused by" describes live behaviour)

Live artifact (final tree, order `205f2991-...` placed through the real stack, `.arm/web29s4/live/causation.out`): status `completed`, **9 timeline entries, 3 show "caused by"**: `order.confirmed.v1` (caused by `credit.approved.v1`), `credit.released.v1` (caused by `payment.received.v1`), `order.completed.v1` (caused by `credit.released.v1`); the other six cite a command (R12/SA-6) and show none. The stage 2 artifact (`.arm/web29s2/live/results.json`, "A completed: causation audit") agrees: 9 entries, `domLinkedCount: 3`, `entriesWithCommandCause: 6`, `commandIdsRenderedAnywhere: false`.

Search of every causation claim (command and one classification per hit):

```
grep -rniE "caused by|causation|caused-by" apps/web/src/app apps/web/src/lib apps/web/src/server --include=*.ts --exclude=*.spec.ts
grep -niE "caused by|causation" README.md apps/web/README.md
```

- UI text: **one** string, `pages/(app)/orders/[id].page.ts:146` "caused by <link to the causing entry's type>", rendered only when `causingEntry` finds an entry of this timeline (`lib/order-detail.ts:98-101`): accurate, and exactly what the live run shows (3 of 9). The other hits in `order-detail.ts` (`:34,56,88,93-96`) and `testing/orders-fixtures.ts` are code comments and fixtures, not user text.
- README `:38` (SA-6 registry row): accurate. `:175` (projector ordering by `causationId`): accurate (ordering, not a UI claim). `:239`, `:241`: history rows ("`credit.released.v1` caused by it", "SA-6"): accurate.
- **README `:184`: INACCURATE, fixed.** It said "caused by" links "Orders' and Billing's own chains". The live timeline shows the links cross contexts: Orders' `order.confirmed.v1` and `order.completed.v1` are caused by Billing's `credit.approved.v1` and `credit.released.v1`, and only `credit.released.v1` <- `payment.received.v1` is inside one context. The paragraph now states the rule, the three live links by name, and that the other six cite a command.

That is a statement about #7 and #8; #9's live run has a Billing-emitted fact (`credit.released.v1`) with a link. Whether #7/#8 differ is not something I measured.

## Stage 4 — follow-up

Scope: the leader's two items. Touched: `services/seed/README.md` (line 21 only), `progress/impl_web_app.md`. No test or source changed. Logs: `.arm/web29s4/loaded.sh`, `loaded.summary`, `loaded_1..5.log`, `final_types:check.log`, `final_build.log`.

### 1. The unexplained `test:ci` failure: not reproduced, test not changed

The failure: `error-text.sweep.spec.ts` > "every reviewed label is produced by some failing run", `reviewed fixture texts that no failing run produces: ... [ 'PRD-0001' ]`, in the first full run only.

What I established by experiment:

- **What state makes that exact assertion fail.** A throwaway probe (`zz-probe.spec.ts`, deleted afterwards) ran the `/stock` baseline with the stock page mutated as arm n101 does (catalogue lookup removed): the baseline then shows `PRD-0001` as the row text (`named=false codeOnlyTexts=1`), so the failing catalogue runs do not ADD that text and the label is "not produced". With the page restored by `cp` (new mtime, same content) the very next run was `named=true codeOnlyTexts=0`, so a stale transform cache after a restore is **ruled out** (2 runs each, before, mutated, restored).
- **Timing of the failing runs.** The probe failed the catalogue read on `/stock` with every status and both variants (8 runs per round): unloaded 3 rounds = 24/24 added `PRD-0001`; with 33 busy-loop processes on 16 cores, 5 rounds = 40/40.
- **Timing of the baseline.** The same `/stock` baseline run 20 times under the same load: 20/20 showed the catalogue name (`named=true`).
- **Loaded full runs of `test:ci`** (24 busy-loop processes on 16 cores, the load process group killed after each run; `loaded.sh`): **5 of 5 passed, 33 files, 667 tests each** (about 580 to 600 s each). Before: 1 failure in 1 full run (the first), then 2 passes unloaded (`test:ci` and the sweep file alone, 119). After (no change made): 5 passes loaded, so 7 passes and 1 failure in 8 full-file runs of the identical tree.

Conclusion: I could not reproduce it in the bounded effort (5 loaded full runs, 40 loaded failing-run probes, 20 loaded baselines), so by the leader's rule I did not change the test. The failure requires the `/stock` baseline to have been taken with the rows already showing the code alone, which is a single memoised event per file (it explains why every one of the eight failing runs missed the label at once); what I could not find is what made the baseline unnamed that one time. The first `test:ci` was started within minutes of the n101 arm, whose mutation and restore touched the same file; I found no mechanism connecting the two and cannot exclude a coincidence. Re-open trigger: the same message in any later run; the first step then is to log `base.text` of the `/stock` baseline when the label premise fails.

"so a live timeline links only the facts Orders itself emits" replaced by the live evidence of section 4 of the stage 4 record: a live completed order (nine entries) shows three "caused by" lines (`order.confirmed.v1` <- `credit.approved.v1`, `credit.released.v1` <- `payment.received.v1`, `order.completed.v1` <- `credit.released.v1`), the links cross contexts, and the other six cite a command. ## Stage 4 — follow-up 2

Scope: the leader's reproduction request for the `PRD-0001` premise failure. Touched: `apps/web/src/app/error-text.sweep.spec.ts` only (test-only; the mechanism is not in product code), and this record. Logs and backups: `.arm/web29s4/` (`cold.sh`, `cold.summary`, `cold_1..6.log`, `sweep.final2.ts`, `gates2.summary`, `g2_*.log`, `arms-f2w.txt`).

### 1. Is a cold cache the condition? No (6 of 6)

Each run cleared `apps/web/node_modules/.vite`, `node_modules/.vitest` and `apps/web/.angular` first, then ran the whole sweep file alone, one at a time (`cold.sh`). A temporary diagnostic printed the `/stock` baseline's final texts, whether its `everShown` contained the bare `PRD-0001`, and what the catalogue-failure runs used.

| Cold run | Result | `/stock` baseline: final texts with the code / `everShown` has bare `PRD-0001` |
|---|---|---|
| 1 to 6 | 119 passed each | `["Olive oil 1 L","(PRD-0001)"]` / false, every run |

A separate probe (6 cold clears, baseline plus the 8 catalogue-failure runs on `/stock`): every cold run, baseline `everShownHasBareCode=false` and all 8 runs (status 500, 502, 503, 504 x detail, title) added `PRD-0001`. So no run missed the text, and I have no failing run to describe (status x variant, added texts, `everShown`) from my own runs; the leader's failing run is the only one observed. Together with the earlier 7 warm passes and 5 loaded passes, that is 18 of 18 passes of this premise on this machine, in my hands.

### 2. The mechanism, shown by experiment (a deterministic version of the race)

`addedTexts(run, base)` compares a failing run's final texts with every text the baseline **ever showed** (`baseline.everShown`, filled by a MutationObserver), not with its final page. The leader's diagnostic printed `base.texts` (the final page), which is why it read "named" while the instrument had still excluded the text. On `/stock` the rows are drawn from the stock list and named from the catalogue; if the stock list lands before the catalogue answer, the baseline shows the bare code `PRD-0001` for a moment before the names arrive. Probe (throwaway spec, deleted): `/stock` mounted with the catalogue answer held on a gate: during the hold `bare=true`; after the release `bare=true named=true`. A baseline with that transient makes the failing runs "add nothing" for `PRD-0001`, for all of them at once (the baseline is memoised once per file), which is exactly the observed pattern: the label is "not produced" by every failing run. What I could not do is make the two answers swap order on their own (they did not in any of my 18 runs), so the cause of the order in the leader's run is inferred from the evidence, not observed; the cache clear is not it.

### 3. The fix (test-only)

- `fixtureTextsShown(run, base)`: a reviewed fixture text counts as produced when the failing run's final page has it and the baseline's **final** page does not. `judge()` (extracted from `failOne`, pure) adds it to `used`. The "extra text beside the problem" check keeps the wider `addedTexts` comparison, which can only be more lenient.
- The staleness message now also prints which fixture texts the baseline ever showed.
- A deterministic unit test (`a fixture text is counted from the baseline's FINAL page, not from what it showed for a moment`) feeds `judge` a baseline caught mid-render. Test count: sweep file 120 (was 119); `test:ci` 668 (was 667).

### 4. Arms on the final tree (`arm.sh`; every `cmp` identical after restore)

| Arm | Mutation | Test | First failure |
|---|---|---|---|
| a1 | `judge` stops adding `fixtureTextsShown` | the new unit test | `AssertionError: expected { Object (used) } to deeply equal { used: ArrayContaining{...} }` |
| p16 (P16) | a label `A label nobody produces` added to `LABELS` | whole sweep file (the premise cannot be armed with `-t`: it times out at 480 s, so the first two `-t` attempts were timeouts, not kills, and were redone) | `reviewed labels that no failing run produces: delete them or fix the page that stopped showing them: expected [ 'A label nobody produces' ] to deeply equal []` |
| p101 (n101) | `/stock` shows the code alone (catalogue lookup removed) | whole sweep file | `reviewed fixture texts that no failing run produces: delete them, or check the /stock baseline, which showed: Order-To-Cash | #9 · Python / Angular | Orders | Stock | Billing | ...` |

### 5. Gates after the last edit (`gates2.sh`)

`types:check` exit 0; `test:ci` from cold caches 33 files, 668 tests passed; `test:ci` warm 33 files, 668 tests passed; `build` exit 0. No docker or listener left (no live run this round).

### 6. Disposition

The condition "cold cache" is refuted by experiment (6 of 6). The guard no longer depends on the order of the two answers on `/stock`. The natural occurrence of that order was not reproduced, so if the premise ever fails again its message now says whether the baseline ever showed the bare code (the first thing to read).
